## Part I. Appendix `GR` — Graphical Registry and 8-Point Passports of All 23 Diagrams and Figures of Book I

Every passport in the present registry contains eight mandatory fields: **(1)** identifier and chapter; **(2)** depicted object and cardinality of the carrier; **(3)** rule of planar projection and layout; **(4)** what is mathematically strict in the drawing; **(5)** what is a convention of the flat page; **(6)** what typical reading error the drawing prevents; **(7)** exact verification gate in `11_verify_full_book_I_v3_3.jl`; **(8)** harmonized reader's caption.

---

### Introduction (`E0`) — `00_INTRODUCTION_FROM_MATHEMATICAL_OBJECT_TO_DISTINCTION_AND_OBSERVER_V3_3_EN.md`

#### Passport `GR-E0-1` (`Diagram 1`: Cylinder, Sphere, Cone, and Two Orthogonal Projection Screens)
1. **Identifier and chapter:** `Diagram 1`, Introduction (`E0`, Part I).
2. **Object and carrier:** finite family of three solid bodies $X_0 = \{\text{sphere},\; \text{right circular cylinder},\; \text{right circular cone}\}$ of common base diameter $D$ and height $D$ with two silhouette projection maps $q_{\mathrm{top}}, q_{\mathrm{side}}$; in Part III expanded to $X_1 = X_0 \cup \{\text{cubic block of side } D\}$.
3. **Exact layout projection:** two-screen orthogonal Monge unfolding (upper screen of the horizontal projection $(x,y)$ and side screen of the frontal projection $(x,z)$ around the object table).
4. **What is strict in the drawing:** coincidence of the circular silhouettes $q_{\mathrm{top}}(\text{sphere}) = q_{\mathrm{top}}(\text{cylinder}) = q_{\mathrm{top}}(\text{cone})$ in the upper window and their complete separation into a circle, a square, and an isosceles triangle in the side window; non-emptiness of the intersection of preimages $q_{\mathrm{top}}^{-1}(\text{circle}) \cap q_{\mathrm{side}}^{-1}(\text{square}) = \{\text{cylinder}\}$.
5. **Convention of the flat page:** ASCII-art rendering of the curvature of a circle and three-dimensional volume of bodies on a character grid; arrows denote geometric projection of rays, **not** transitions of an automaton in time.
6. **Prevented error:** identifying coincidence of visible shadows in a single window ($q_1(x) = q_1(y)$) with identity of the distinguished objects themselves ($x = y$), as well as attributing incompatible silhouettes to "contradictoriness of the body itself" without specifying the reading vantage point.
7. **`Julia` gate:** `GATE-I-E0` (verification of partitions of a finite window and the Boolean subalgebra of preimages $2^{|Y|}$).
8. **Reader's caption:** object mini-scene of two orthogonal observation windows: three distinct bodies are indistinguishable in the top view and are unambiguously separated when the side screen is connected, without changing the bodies themselves.

#### Passport `GR-E0-2` (`Diagram 2`: Semantic Map of Three Entry Points into Mathematical Description)
1. **Identifier and chapter:** `Diagram 2`, Introduction (`E0`, Part I).
2. **Object and carrier:** methodological comparison table of three starting positions of formalization: set-theoretic (ready-made elements $x \in X$), structural-categorical (ready-made environment of morphisms $\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(A,B)$), and operational-projective (reading scene $\Gamma = (X, \mathcal{Q}, \mathcal{A}, \ldots)$ with an external position $\mathcal{O} \notin X$).
3. **Exact layout projection:** three-column parallel table from the primary question to derived constructions.
4. **What is strict in the drawing:** direction of logical dependency in the third column: first an admissible question $q : X \to Y$ and its equivalence kernel $\mathrm{Eq}(q)$ are fixed, and only then does the quotient carrier $X/\mathrm{Eq}(q)$ acquire the status of a derived mathematical object.
5. **Convention of the flat page:** rectangular boxes denote epistemological levels of problem formulation, not nodes of a transition graph or a commutative diagram of morphisms.
6. **Prevented error:** perceiving DOT as an "abolition of classical set theory or category theory", whereas in reality DOT investigates the operational threshold prior to which a ready-made set of distinguishable states and a class of morphisms still have to be explicitly carved out of a stream of readings.
7. **`Julia` gate:** `GATE-I-E0` and `GATE-I01-A` (primacy of $\mathrm{Eq}(q)$ over label renaming across `32` and `1458` tests).
8. **Reader's caption:** comparison of three starting points of mathematical discourse—from a ready-made object, from a ready-made environment of arrows, and from an operational act of distinction.

#### Passport `GR-E0-3` (`Diagram 3`: Room, Active Camera Vantage Point, and Partial Mirror)
1. **Identifier and chapter:** `Diagram 3`, Introduction (`E0`, Part II).
2. **Object and carrier:** finite model of partial internal self-report $X = D \times M = \{0,1\}^2$ with trace-reading channel $q(d, m) = m \in \{0,1\}$ contrasted against the full set of all Boolean questions $\mathcal{P}(X)$ ($|\mathcal{P}(X)| = 2^{|X|} > |X|$).
3. **Exact layout projection:** plan view of the room from above: field of view of the lens $D$, partial mirror on the far wall $M$, and support point of the active camera outside the current frame.
4. **What is strict in the drawing:** simultaneous truth of two statements without paradox: (a) existence of a non-trivial internal memory channel $q : D \times M \to M$ reading its own recorded trace; (b) absence of a surjection $X \twoheadrightarrow \mathcal{P}(X)$ by the Cantor–Lawvere theorem (verified by exhaustive search of all $4^4 = 256$ maps $\mathrm{Fin}\,2 \to \mathcal{P}(\mathrm{Fin}\,2)$, yielding `0` surjections).
5. **Convention of the flat page:** the geometric cone of rays depicts the domain of definition of a concrete act of reading, not the physical optics of a real instrument.
6. **Prevented error:** a twofold extreme—either naive belief in "complete self-capture" of a system together with all questions about it inside a single frame, or the false conclusion that the prohibition of complete self-capture forbids any finite reflection and self-check via a trace in a mirror.
7. **`Julia` gate:** `GATE-I-E0` (witness $q(d,m)=m$ on $\{0,1\}^2$ and exhaustive check of `256` Cantor–Lawvere maps with `0` surjections).
8. **Reader's caption:** distinction between illegitimate complete self-capture of the entire scene and admissible partial self-report via an internal trace register $M$.

#### Passport `GR-E0-4` (`Diagram 4`: Lever Balance and Three Necessary Supports of an Act of Comparison)
1. **Identifier and chapter:** `Diagram 4`, Introduction (`E0`, Part II).
2. **Object and carrier:** four-input Boolean gate of joint necessity $y(s, r, t, x) = s \cdot r \cdot t \cdot x$ on $\{0,1\}^4$ (`16` input tuples) vs. the degenerate gate $y'(s, r, x) = s \cdot r \cdot x$ independent of $t$.
3. **Exact layout projection:** frontal diagram of an equal-arm beam with a left pan for the reference standard ($r$), a right pan for the tested load ($x$), a central pivot pin ($s$), and free pointer travel with a reading scale ($t$).
4. **What is strict in the drawing:** collapse of the informational output to a constant upon zeroing out any of the three coupling conditions ($s = 0$, $r = 0$, or $t = 0$), i.e., impossibility of reducing the three-place support of comparison to the pair of weights alone.
5. **Convention of the flat page:** mechanical details of the beam serve as a visual carrier of the logical product of admissibility conditions, not as a model of continuous lever statics.
6. **Prevented error:** reducing an act of distinction to the two compared sides while forgetting the third binding condition (common support, coupling to the scale, and admissible comparison travel).
7. **`Julia` gate:** `GATE-I-E0` (exhaustive check of all `16` inputs of the gate $y = s r t x$ verifying the essentiality of each factor $s, r, t$ against the insensitivity of $y' = s r x$ to $t$).
8. **Reader's caption:** visual anatomy of the hidden triadic nature of comparison: two pans are distinguished only under simultaneous operation of the fixed fulcrum, free beam travel, and reading scale.

#### Passport `GR-I.1.1` (`Fig. I.1.1`: Four-State Polar Cell $S_4$, Involutions $\sigma / \mathrm{swap}$, and Cycle $C_4$)
1. **Identifier and chapter:** `Fig. I.1.1`, Chapter `I.1` (`§I.1.1`–`§I.1.2`).

5. **Convention of the flat page:** the intersection of the two antipodal diagonals of $\sigma$ at the center of the square in panel (b) is a purely projective crossing of segments on paper and does **not** form a fifth vertex of the graph.
6. **Prevented error:** confusing the full skeleton of all pairwise distinctions $K_4 = (\Delta^3)^{(1)}$ (`6` edges, symmetry group $S_4$ of order `24`) with the single-bit flip graph $Q_2$ (`4` edges, automorphism group $D_4$ of order `8`), confusing the involutions $\sigma$ and $\mathrm{swap}$, and the illusion that the diagonal question $\beta = x \oplus c$ is "more secondary" than the coordinate questions $q_x, q_c$.
7. **`Julia` gate:** `GATE-I01-B` (verification of $|X/\mathrm{Eq}(q_{12})|=4$ on `24/256` pairs of questions, bijectivity of decoders $d_c, d_x$, fixed points of $\sigma$ (`0`) vs. $\mathrm{swap}$ (`2`), and the triple of `2+2` partitions).
8. **Reader's caption:** three operational slices of the four-state cell $S_4$: the square of single-bit switches $Q_2$, the contrast between the free antipodal involution $\sigma$ and the axial exchange $\mathrm{swap}$, and the 4-cycle $C_4$ with the bipartite partition of the comparison question $\beta = x \oplus c$.

#### Passport `GR-I.1.2` (`Fig. I.1.2`: Gluing Two Cells $S_4$ Along a Common Support Seam $c$ vs. Disjoint Union)
1. **Identifier and chapter:** `Fig. I.1.2`, Chapter `I.1` (`§I.1.3`).
2. **Object and carrier:** fibered product (pullback) $S_4 \times_{\mathbb{F}_2} S_4 = \{((x_L, c), (x_R, c)) : x_L, x_R, c \in \mathbb{F}_2\}$ (`8` admissible joint states, `12` unit-step edges—the cube $Q_3$) contrasted with the direct product $S_4 \times S_4$ (`16` pairs without a seam) and the disjoint sum of two squares $2Q_2 = Q_2 \sqcup Q_2$ (`8` vertices, `8` edges).
3. **Exact layout projection:** two-story prism over the common two-valued seam $c \in \{0, 1\}$: the lower square corresponds to the fiber $c = 0$ (`4` pairs $(x_L, x_R)$), the upper square to the fiber $c = 1$ (`4` pairs $(x_L, x_R)$), and the four vertical struts to the synchronized switching of the common support $c \mapsto c \oplus 1$.
4. **What is strict in the drawing:** reduction of the number of states from $4 \times 4 = 16$ to $|S_4 \times_{\mathbb{F}_2} S_4| = 2 \times 2 + 2 \times 2 = 8$; presence of exactly `4` vertical gluing edges turning $2Q_2$ (`8` edges) into the connected cube $Q_3$ (`12` edges); order of the cube automorphism group $|\mathrm{Aut}(Q_3)| = 48$ and exact orders of the subgroups preserving the distinguished seam structure (`[8, 4, 16, 8]`).
5. **Convention of the flat page:** oblique axonometry of the three-dimensional cube $Q_3$ on the plane (inequality of paper lengths of frontal and slanted edges despite their complete combinatorial equality $d_{\mathrm{H}} = 1$).
6. **Prevented error:** twofold confusion at the junction of subsystems—either replacing the fibered product $L \times_K R$ by the full Cartesian product $L \times R$ (which permits desynchronization $c_L \neq c_R$), or substituting cofibered gluing (a pushout of cardinality `6` or a disjoint union of cardinality `8` with `8` edges) for the pullback.
7. **`Julia` gate:** `GATE-I01-B` (`|S_4 ×_{F_2} S_4| = 8` vs. `16`, `2Q_2` `8` edges vs. `Q_3` `12` edges, pushout `6`, colimit `2`, subgroups of orders `[8, 4, 16, 8]` in the group of order `48`).
8. **Reader's caption:** gluing two four-state cells along a common support channel $c$: seam compatibility carves out from `16` pairs exactly `8` joint states and binds the two fibers $c=0$ and $c=1$ by four vertical struts into a single cube $Q_3$.

### Chapter `I.2` — `02_CHAPTER_I_02_PARTIAL_ACTIONS_RELATIONS_AND_QUESTION_TRANSFER_V3_3_EN.md`

3. **Exact layout projection:** two-panel diagram in `Fig. I.4.1`: on the left, the reversible cascade $T_{\mathrm{rev}}$ feeding the old bit $z$ into a modulo-`2` adder (`z' = z ⊕ x ⊕ c`, bijection `8 → 8`); on the right, the erasing cascade $T_{\mathrm{ret}}$ dumping the old value $z$ into an erasure sink (`z' = x ⊕ c`, compression `8 → 4` with cost $C_{\mathrm{erase}} = 1$ bit).
4. **What is strict in the drawing:** non-equivalence of an open-loop program tape and closed-loop feedback control with reading under an unknown initial state (already on `Fin 2`); preservation of all `8` states and the exact equality $T_{\mathrm{rev}}^4 = \mathrm{id}_{S_8}$ under the reversible step $T_{\mathrm{rev}}$ vs. collapsing pairs $(x,c,0)$ and $(x,c,1)$ into a single image under the erasing step $T_{\mathrm{ret}}$; pairwise irreducibility of the five coordinates of the cost vector.
5. **Convention of the flat page:** spatial separation of the signal line $x$, support line $c$, and trace register line $z$ in the diagram, whereas mathematically the step acts on the single eight-element product $S_8 = \{0,1\}^3$.
6. **Prevented error:** three engineering-mathematical substitutions—(a) confusing Nerode future compression ($X \twoheadrightarrow X/{\sim_\Gamma}$, which forgets the past for the sake of the future) with Bennett–Toffoli reversible extension ($S_4 \hookrightarrow S_4 \times \mathbb{F}_2$, which retains a trace for the sake of reversibility of the past); (b) transferring an abstract quotient step for free without checking realizability in a restricted library $\Omega$ (exact bounds $C_{\min}(\mathrm{A}) = 11$, $C_{\min}(\mathrm{B}) = 19$ by the general formula $2M+5$, and constructive automata $Q_{11}, Q_{19}, Q_{22}$ executing `139` commands); (c) reducing five heterogeneous cost items to a single arbitrary scalar.

1. **Identifier and chapter:** `Fig. I.6.2`, Chapter `I.6` (`§I.6.3`).
2. **Object and carrier:** on the left—the Fano projective plane $\mathrm{PG}(2,2) = (\mathbb{F}_2^3 \setminus \{0\}, \mathcal{L}_7)$ of `7` points and `7` three-point lines with collinearity condition $u \oplus v \oplus w = 0$; on the right—its bipartite Levi incidence graph (the Heawood graph $H_{14}$: `7 + 7 = 14` vertices, `21` edges, degree `3`, girth `6`, $|\mathrm{Aut}(\mathrm{PG}(2,2))| = |\mathrm{GL}_3(2)| = 168$, $|\mathrm{Aut}(H_{14})| = 336$).
3. **Exact layout projection:** on the left—the classic equilateral triangle with three medians and the inscribed circle of the seventh line; on the right—the bipartite unfolding "points $P_1..P_7$ vs. lines $\ell_1..\ell_7$" (`21` incidence edges).
4. **What is strict in the drawing:** every block $\ell \in \mathcal{L}_7$ contains exactly `3` points, through every point pass exactly `3` lines, any two distinct points lie on a unique line, and any two lines intersect in a unique point; the incidence matrix $\delta_7 \in \{0,1\}^{7\times 7}$ satisfies $\delta_7 \delta_7^\top = 2I_7 + J_7$ over $\mathbb{Z}$ ($|\det_{\mathbb{Z}} \delta_7| = 24$) and has rank `4` over $\mathbb{F}_2$ (the kernel $W = \ker\delta_7$ of dimension `3` forms the `[7,3,4]_2` simplex code, the image $C = \mathrm{im}\,\delta_7$ of dimension `4` forms the `[7,4,3]_2` Hamming code, and the surjection $\delta_4 = j^{-1} \circ \delta_7 : \mathbb{F}_2^7 \twoheadrightarrow \mathbb{F}_2^4$ admits exactly $|\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{F}_2}(\mathbb{F}_2^4, W)| = 2^{4\cdot 3} = 4096$ linear right inverse sections $s : \mathbb{F}_2^4 \to \mathbb{F}_2^7$ with $\delta_4 \circ s = \mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{F}_2^4}$).
5. **Convention of the flat page:** by the Sylvester–Gallai theorem, the Fano configuration cannot be embedded in the real Euclidean plane $\mathbb{R}^2$ by seven straight-line segments without extraneous crossings, so one of the seven equally rightful projective lines is depicted by an inscribed circle.
6. **Prevented error:** perceiving the "circular" Fano line as a special curved object differing from the six straight segments (the group $\mathrm{GL}_3(2)$ acts transitively on all seven lines), expecting non-singularity of the matrix $\delta_7$ over $\mathbb{F}_2$ by analogy with $\mathbb{Q}$, and confusing the surjection $\delta_4 : \mathbb{F}_2^7 \twoheadrightarrow \mathbb{F}_2^4$ (`4096` sections) with the syndrome operator $\nu_W = J_7 \oplus \delta_7$ of rank `3`.
7. **`Julia` gate:** `GATE-I06-B` (Fano book in $\mathrm{PG}(3,2)$, rank `4` over $\mathbb{F}_2$, $|\det_{\mathbb{Z}} \delta_7| = 24$, `24` perfect matchings of the Heawood graph, exact sequence $\nu_W \circ \delta_7 = 0$, and explicit construction of all `4096` right inverse sections $\delta_4 \circ s = I_4$).
8. **Reader's caption:** the minimal finite projective plane $\mathrm{PG}(2,2)$ of seven non-zero vectors of $\mathbb{F}_2^3$ and seven lines $u \oplus v \oplus w = 0$ (left) and its exact bipartite unfolding without curved lines—the Heawood graph $H_{14}$ (right).

#### Passport `GR-I.6.3` (`Fig. I.6.3`: Petersen Graph $KG(5,2)$ and Its Two-Sheeted Bipartite Desargues Cover $\Gamma_{\mathrm{Des}}$)
1. **Identifier and chapter:** `Fig. I.6.3`, Chapter `I.6` (`§I.6.3`).
2. **Object and carrier:** Kneser graph $KG(5,2)$ (`10` vertices—all `2`-subsets of $\{1,2,3,4,5\}$, `15` edges by the disjointness condition $A \cap B = \varnothing$, regular of degree `3`, girth `5`, diameter `2`, $\chi = 3$), isomorphic to the orthogonality graph on the `10` non-singular points $V_{10} = \{x \in \mathbb{F}_2^4 \setminus \{0\} : q_-(x) = 1\}$ of an elliptic quadric in the symplectic space $W(3,2)$ minus a five-point ovoid $O_5$, and its two-sheeted bipartite cover—the Desargues graph $\Gamma_{\mathrm{Des}} = KG(5,2) \times K_2$ (direct categorical product in $\mathbf{Grph}$ with an edge $K_2$: `20` vertices, `30` edges, girth `6`, $\chi = 2$).
3. **Exact layout projection:** concentric layout with $D_5$ symmetry: outer regular pentagon `{12, 34, 15, 23, 45}`, inner vertices `{35, 25, 24, 14, 13}` (connected by five radial spokes `12—35, 34—25, 15—24, 23—14, 45—13` to the outer cycle and forming an inner five-pointed star `35—14—25—13—24—35` of step `2`), and diagram of splitting each vertex $v \in KG(5,2)$ into a pair of sheets $(v, 0), (v, 1) \in \Gamma_{\mathrm{Des}} = KG(5,2) \times K_2$.
4. **What is strict in the drawing:** strongly regular matrix identity $A_{\mathrm{P}}^2 + A_{\mathrm{P}} - 2I_{10} = J_{10}$ (spectrum $3^1, 1^5, (-2)^4$, projector of rank `5`); non-planarity and absence of a Hamiltonian cycle in $KG(5,2)$; absence of a section homomorphism $KG(5,2) \to \Gamma_{\mathrm{Des}}$ ($\chi(KG(5,2))=3 > 2 = \chi(\Gamma_{\mathrm{Des}})$); failure of natural role quotient descent $KG(5,2) \not\to O_6$ when gluing two elements of the five-element list (the pair `{4,5}` degenerates into a singleton, while adjacent pairs `{1,3}` and `{2,5}` map to antipodal non-adjacent vertices of $O_6$) alongside the existence of an abstract 3-chromatic homomorphism $KG(5,2) \to K_3 \hookrightarrow O_6$.
5. **Convention of the flat page:** separating the `10` vertices into an "outer pentagon" and an "inner star" is a choice of subgroup $D_5 \subset S_5$ for the planar drawing; in the full automorphism group $S_5$ of order `120`, all `10` vertices and all `15` edges are equivalent, and crossings of star rays at the center are **not** vertices.
6. **Prevented error:** falsely inferring from the existence of the local covering $\pi_{\mathrm{Des}} : \Gamma_{\mathrm{Des}} \twoheadrightarrow KG(5,2)$ the existence of a global section $KG(5,2) \hookrightarrow \Gamma_{\mathrm{Des}}$, writing the direct product $KG(5,2) \times K_2$ as a fibered seam $\times_{K_2}$ without maps to a base, and naively attempting to "glue two vertices of the five-element list" believing that the disjoint-pair graph will automatically pass into the octahedron $O_6$.
7. **`Julia` gate:** `GATE-I06-B` ($KG(5,2)$ on $V_{10} = W(3,2) \setminus O_5$, $A_{\mathrm{P}}^2 + A_{\mathrm{P}} - 2I_{10} = J_{10}$, Desargues cover $KG(5,2) \times K_2$ of `20` vertices and `30` edges, failure of natural gluing `KG(5,2) !-> O_6` vs. 3-coloring `KG(5,2) -> K_3 -> O_6`).
8. **Reader's caption:** the Petersen graph $KG(5,2)$ as the graph of disjoint pairs of five elements (left) and its two-sheeted bipartite Desargues unfolding $\Gamma_{\mathrm{Des}} = KG(5,2) \times K_2$ (right), separating odd pentagonal cycles across two sign sheets.

### Chapter `I.7` — `07_CHAPTER_I_07_ADDITION_AND_MULTIPLICATION_ADDITIVE_AND_DIVISOR_QUESTIONS_V3_3_EN.md`

#### Passport `GR-I.7.1` (`Fig. I.7.1`: Four Numbers $\{1, 2, 3, 6\}$ Under Additive and Divisor Questions)

2. **Object and carrier:** four-element numerical carrier $V = \{1, 2, 3, 6\}$ under two binary adjacency questions: (a) additive step $|u - v| = 1$ (graph $G_{\mathrm{add}} \cong P_3 \sqcup K_1$, `2` edges, `2` connected components); (b) immediate Hasse divisor covering $u \prec_{\mathrm{div}} v \iff u \mid v$ and $v/u$ is prime (graph $G_{\mathrm{div}} \cong C_4 = Q_2$, `4` edges, connected 2-regular square).
3. **Exact layout projection:** two-panel comparison on the fixed label set `{1, 2, 3, 6}`: on the left, the linear additive scale with a gap between `3` and `6`; on the right, the two-dimensional Hasse lattice along the prime-power axes $2^a 3^b$ ($a, b \in \{0, 1\}$).
4. **What is strict in the drawing:** non-isomorphism $G_{\mathrm{add}} \not\cong G_{\mathrm{div}}$ on one and the same set of numbers (`2` edges vs. `4`, isolated vertex `6` vs. degree `2` at all vertices); isomorphism of the lattice $D(6)$ to the Boolean square $\mathcal{P}(\{2, 3\}) \cong Q_2$.
5. **Convention of the flat page:** equal distances between nodes in the right panel reflect elementary prime steps ($\times 2$ and $\times 3$), not differences on the number line ($6 - 3 = 3 \neq 2 - 1 = 1$).
6. **Prevented error:** perceiving "proximity of natural numbers" as a single intrinsic property of the number itself apart from the chosen comparison operation ($+1$ vs. $\times p$).
7. **`Julia` gate:** `GATE-I07-A` (verification of edge counts, degrees, and connected components of $G_{\mathrm{add}}$ and $G_{\mathrm{div}}$ on $\{1, 2, 3, 6\}$).
8. **Reader's caption:** change of the connectivity graph on the four numbers $\{1, 2, 3, 6\}$: a disconnected chain with an isolated six in the additive window and a closed square $Q_2$ in the divisor window.

#### Passport `GR-I.7.2` (`Fig. I.7.2`: Boolean Divisor Lattice $D(30) \cong Q_3$ vs. Non-Boolean Lattices $D(12)$ and $D(45)$)
1. **Identifier and chapter:** `Fig. I.7.2`, Chapter `I.7` (`§I.7.2`).
2. **Object and carrier:** Hasse diagrams of three divisor lattices with operations $(\gcd, \operatorname{lcm})$ and complementary divisor involution $\nu_N(d) = N/d$: (a) squarefree number $N = 30 = 2 \cdot 3 \cdot 5$ (`8` divisors, cube $Q_3$); (b) number with a squared primary floor $N = 12 = 2^2 \cdot 3$ (`6` divisors, grid $\{0,1,2\} \times \{0,1\}$); (c) odd squareful number $N = 45 = 3^2 \cdot 5$ (`6` divisors, grid $\{0,1,2\} \times \{0,1\}$).
3. **Exact layout projection:** Hasse diagrams graded from bottom to top by the sum of prime exponents $\Omega(d)$ (from `1` on the bottom floor to $N$ on the top floor).
4. **What is strict in the drawing:** equivalence of Booleanness of the lattice $D(N)$ to squarefreeness of the number $N$ ($\mu(N) \neq 0$): in $D(30)$, for every $d \mid 30$ one has $\gcd(d, 30/d) = 1$ and $\operatorname{lcm}(d, 30/d) = 30$, whereas in $D(12)$ on the middle floor $d = 2$ we have $\nu_{12}(2) = 6$, $\gcd(2, 6) = 2 \neq 1$, $\operatorname{lcm}(2, 6) = 6 \neq 12$, and in $D(45)$ on the floor $d = 3$ we have $\nu_{45}(3) = 15$, $\gcd(3, 15) = 3 \neq 1$, $\operatorname{lcm}(3, 15) = 15 \neq 45$ (the elements `2` in $D(12)$ and `3` in $D(45)$ have no lattice complement at all).
5. **Convention of the flat page:** in the planar projection of the cube $D(30)$, edges of different prime directions ($\times 2, \times 3, \times 5$) have different slopes on the page, even though in the Boolean algebra $\mathcal{P}(\{2,3,5\})$ all three atoms are symmetric.
6. **Prevented error:** mechanically transferring the Boolean complement law $\gcd(d, N/d) = 1$ from squarefree moduli to arbitrary numbers containing prime squares $p^2 \mid N$.
7. **`Julia` gate:** `GATE-I07-A` (complete verification of complements in $D(6), D(30)$ and absence of complements for middle floors in $D(12), D(45)$ across all $N \le 200$).
8. **Reader's caption:** geometric difference between the squarefree number $30$ (the cube $Q_3$, where the pair $(d, 30/d)$ is always coprime) and the numbers $12$ and $45$ with an intermediate squared floor that destroys Boolean complementation.

#### Passport `GR-I.8.1` (`Fig. I.8.1`: Hidden Torus $\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z} \times \mathbb{Z}/q\mathbb{Z}$ and the Oracle-Free Contract on $N = 77$)
1. **Identifier and chapter:** `Fig. I.8.1`, Chapter `I.8` (`§I.8.2`).
2. **Object and carrier:** residue ring $\mathbb{Z}/77\mathbb{Z}$ of cardinality `77` for the semiprime modulus $N = 77 = 7 \cdot 11$ under the canonical CRT isomorphism $\Phi : \mathbb{Z}/77\mathbb{Z} \xrightarrow{\sim} \mathbb{Z}/7\mathbb{Z} \times \mathbb{Z}/11\mathbb{Z}$ in two access regimes: the hidden two-dimensional `7 × 11` grid (inaccessible to direct reading without knowing $p, q$) and the open oracle-free Euclidean probe $g(r) = \gcd(77, r)$.
3. **Exact layout projection:** on the left—the `7 × 11` table of remainders $(r \bmod 7, r \bmod 11)$ with the highlighted cross of the two zero coordinate axes $r \equiv 0 \pmod 7$ and $r \equiv 0 \pmod{11}$; on the right—the three open value classes of the open probe $g(r) \in \{1, 7, 11, 77\}$.
4. **What is strict in the drawing:** exact partition `77 = 1 + 6 + 10 + 60`: the origin $(0,0)$ yields the trivial answer $\gcd(77, 0) = 77$ (`1` point), the `60` invertible elements $(\mathbb{Z}/77\mathbb{Z})^\times$ outside both axes yield the silent answer $\gcd(77, r) = 1$ ($\varphi(77) = 6 \cdot 10 = 60$), and exactly `6 + 10 = 16` non-zero axial points uncover the non-trivial prime divisors `7` (`10` points of the axis $r \equiv 0 \pmod 7, r \not\equiv 0 \pmod{11}$) and `11` (`6` points of the axis $r \equiv 0 \pmod{11}, r \not\equiv 0 \pmod 7$).
5. **Convention of the flat page:** in the left panel, the hidden torus is already unfolded into a neat rectangular grid along the axes $7$ and $11$ for the reader's eye, whereas in the actual input to an algorithm all `77` remainders are interleaved on the one-dimensional ring $0, 1, \dots, 76$ without row and column labels.
6. **Prevented error:** the methodological self-deception of "peeking at the answer" (`oracle leak`), when in evaluating or designing a divisor-search procedure on a composite modulus one imperceptibly substitutes the operation $r \bmod p$ or a ready-made coordinate on the hidden torus into a computation step before the open probe $\gcd(N, r)$ has hit one of the two axes.
7. **`Julia` gate:** `GATE-I08-A` (verification of CRT bijection on $N=45$, failure on $(4,6)$, exact decomposition `1 + 16 + 60 = 77` on $N=77$, and oracle-free contract).
8. **Reader's caption:** geometric anatomy of oracle-free observation of the composite modulus $N = 77$: `60` interior points of the torus return one under Euclid's probe, and only hitting the `16` non-zero points of the hidden axial cross legitimately exhibits a divisor.

#### Passport `GR-I.8.2` (`Fig. I.8.2`: Hankel Matrix $H(a)_{i,j} = a_{i+j}$ as a Table of Shifts of a Single Stream of Observations)
1. **Identifier and chapter:** `Fig. I.8.2`, Chapter `I.8` (`§I.8.4`).
2. **Object and carrier:** infinite (and its finite leading slices $H_m(a)$ of size $m \times m$) Hankel matrix of a sequence $a = (a_0, a_1, a_2, \dots)$ over a field $K$, whose rows are successive shifts $S^i a = (a_i, a_{i+1}, a_{i+2}, \dots)$, while all entries on each antidiagonal $i + j = s$ equal one and the same measurement $a_s$.
3. **Exact layout projection:** square $4 \times 4$ window of entries $(a_{i+j})_{0 \le i,j \le 3}$ with explicit labeling of rows as shift states $S^0 a, S^1 a, S^2 a, S^3 a$ and columns as future delay questions $q_0, q_1, q_2, q_3$.

5. **Convention of the flat page:** the finite top-left $4 \times 4$ corner of the infinite table is depicted; equality of the rank of a finite window to the rank of the entire infinite matrix is reached only after passing all transient delays and any accidental vanishings of intermediate minors modulo $p$.
6. **Prevented error:** two subtle miscalculations—(a) forgetting the contribution of the numerator degree $\deg P + 1$ in the presence of an initial transient segment, and (b) stopping rank computation at the first vanishing leading minor $\Delta_k \equiv 0 \pmod p$ (such as $\Delta_2 = 14 \equiv 0 \pmod 7$ for $s_n = 1^n + 2^n + 4^n$, even though the true rank over $\mathbb{F}_7$ equals `3` because $\Delta_3 = 36 \equiv 1 \pmod 7$).
7. **`Julia` gate:** `GATE-I08-B` (rank `2` of the transient impulse $(0,1,0,\dots)$, rank `2` of the Fibonacci series, minors `[3, 14, 36, 0]`, and modular ranks `[1, 2, 3, 3]` over $\mathbb{F}_2, \mathbb{F}_3, \mathbb{F}_5, \mathbb{F}_7$ for $1^n + 2^n + 4^n$).
8. **Reader's caption:** the Hankel matrix as the meeting of past shifts and future delay windows: the number of linearly independent rows in it exactly measures the dimension of the hidden linear memory of the stream.

## Part II. Appendix `VP` — How to Verify the Book Independently, Registry of 56 Formal Specifications, and Certification Registry of 57 Statements

### II.1. How to Verify the Book Independently: Execution, Pass Criterion, and Verification Architecture

**Execution.** Install `Julia 1.12` (standard library only), navigate to the volume directory, and run:

```bash
JULIA_NUM_THREADS=1 OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS=1 julia --threads=1 11_verify_full_book_I_v3_3.jl
```

**Pass criterion.** The script prints one line per gate and at the end outputs the summary `23 / 23 GATES PASS` (`19` mathematical gates covering chapters `E0` and `I.1–I.9`, plus `4` system-text gates `GATE-I-SYS-*`); simultaneously it updates the certificate `12_book_I_v3_3_master_certificate.json` with the `Julia` version, timestamp, and detailed breakdown of every gate. Any other result indicates that the text or computation of the volume diverges from what is stated, and the line of the corresponding gate identifies the point of divergence.

**Verification architecture.**

All computational and structural verification of Volume I is concentrated in the single reproducible script `11_verify_full_book_I_v3_3.jl`, generating the final machine certificate `12_book_I_v3_3_master_certificate.json` under the following strict constraints:

1. **Single-threaded mode without memory races:** `JULIA_NUM_THREADS=1`, `OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS=1`, `BLAS.set_num_threads(1)`, standard library of `Julia 1.12.6` only (`LinearAlgebra`, `SHA`, `Dates`).
2. **Complete exclusion of floating-point approximations (`0` `Float64` / `Float32` / `BigFloat`):** all computations are performed exclusively in exact integer (`Int`, `BigInt`), exact rational (`Rational{Int}`, `Rational{BigInt}`), and modular ($\mathbb{F}_2, \mathbb{F}_3, \mathbb{F}_4, \mathbb{F}_9, \mathbb{F}_p$) arithmetic; the absence of floating-point types is verified by a dedicated lexical and type gate `GATE-I-SYS-FLOAT`.
3. **Separation of roles between finite verification and general deduction:** the finite gates `GATE-I*` exhaustively certify finite tables, combinatorial invariants, counterexamples, and finite windows of theorems, but **do not substitute for** analytic proofs on infinite carriers (all finite sets $X$, free modules $\mathbb{Z}[X]$, the natural series $\mathbb{N}$, and rational series $K[[t]]$), which are given in full in the main text of the chapters.
4. **Twenty-three verification gates (`19` mathematical + `4` system-text):**
   - `GATE-I-E0` — introductory finite models of the Introduction (`F(x)=1-x`, Bell tolerance on `0, 3/5, 6/5`, gate `y = s r t x`, partial self-report vs. Cantor–Lawvere `0/16` and `0/256` surjections, Boolean subalgebra $2^{|Y|}$);
   - `GATE-I01-A`, `GATE-I01-B` — operational contract, primacy of $\mathrm{Eq}(q)$, countermodels $(Z_D, Z_C, Z_F)$, `256` binary relations on `2` points (`16` functions, `4` bijections), clipping homomorphism `min(x,1)` vs. failure of quotient descent of the saturating semigroup $(\{0,1,2,3\}, \oplus_{\mathrm{sat}})$, four-state cell $S_4$, decoders $d_c, d_x$, pullback $|S_4 \times_{\mathbb{F}_2} S_4| = 8$, groups `48 / 16 / 8`, and Belnap lattice $\mathcal{F}_4$;
   - `GATE-I02-A`, `GATE-I02-B` — category $\mathbf{Par}$, Galois connection $\exists_R \dashv \forall_R$ (`256/256`), Lawvere triad (and counterexample of non-surjective $\pi : \{0\} \to \{0,1\}$) and Beck–Chevalley counterexample, Zhegalkin filtration `16 → 8 → 6`, holonomy $H_3$ (`3200 / 2688 / 2560`), and duality $F_\alpha / F_\alpha^\top$ over $\mathbb{Q}$;
   - `GATE-I03-A`, `GATE-I03-B` — six-point combinatorial carrier $V_6$ (`4 → 5 → 6`), Nerode algorithm and duality $\mathrm{Atoms}(B_k) = X/{\sim_k}$ across `256` total, `625` partial, and `6561` Mealy automata, cylindrical closure (`16 vs 32`, `64 vs 65`), 7-state counterexample of trace equivalence without lumpability and counterexample $\mathrm{TV}=1$, six-bit testbed `4, 6, 8, 8` on `64` states, $K_2 \square P_3$, separation of measures on `256` pairs, and classification `256 = 64 + 96 + 96` (`96 = 48 + 36 + 12`);
   - `GATE-I04-A`, `GATE-I04-B` — reversible trace gate $T_{\mathrm{rev}}$ vs. $T_{\mathrm{ret}}$, explicit simulation of the 11-state class `A` controller $Q_{11}$ ($L \ge 3$, $4$ ticks), 19-state class `B` controller $Q_{19}$ via two $3\times 3$ cyclic Latin squares ($L \ge 3$, $\le 3$ ticks, `139` commands, bound $C_{\min}(D_M) = 2M + 5$), and 22-state class `A` controller $Q_{22}$ ($L = 9$, $\le 3$ ticks, `139` commands) on the plant $P_a(x, y) = (x - y + a, x)$ on $D^2$, equivariant three-layer decoding of the cut coboundary of $K_4$ `[6, 3, 3]_2` under `384` signed-permutation symmetries, exact Markov coupling over `ℚ`, cycle potential gauge, and reset monoid across `19 683` triples;
   - `GATE-I05-A`, `GATE-I05-B` — dipole and free module $\mathbb{Z}[X]$, shells `1+6+12+8=27` and `13` axes of $\{-1,0,+1\}^3$, monotonicity of `256` Boolean pairs and shutters vs. `Ban-Go` cancellation and signed path revival, quarter-turn $J_{\mathbb{Z}}$, and even Hadamard sublattice $\mathcal{L}$;
   - `GATE-I06-A`, `GATE-I06-B` — graphs on $A_6$, $L(Q_3)$ vs. $L(O_6)$, bicycle code `[12, 3, 6]_2`, plane $\mathrm{AG}(2,3)$ and fields $\mathbb{F}_3, \mathbb{F}_4$, constructive generation of three groups of order `48` (including the binary octahedral group $2O \subset \mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb{F}_9)$ with `1` involution vs. `19` in $W(B_3)$ and `13` in $\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb{F}_7)$), Fano plane $\mathrm{PG}(2,2)$, all `4096` right inverse sections of the surjection $\delta_4 = j^{-1} \circ \delta_7$ and syndrome $\nu_W = J_7 \oplus \delta_7$ of rank `3`, Petersen graph $KG(5,2)$ on $W(3,2) \setminus O_5$, Klein correspondence `35 / 105`, Desargues cover $KG(5,2) \times K_2$, permutation roots `0 vs 12`, orbits `20 / 12 / 10`, and failure $KG(5,2) \not\to O_6$;
   - `GATE-I07-A`, `GATE-I07-B` — graphs on `{1,2,3,6}`, lattices $D(6), D(30), D(12), D(45)$, balance $m = \pi(2m) + c(2m)$ and window `10, 12, 14`, equation $x+y+z=xyz$ in $\mathbb{N}^3$ and $\mathbb{Z}^3$, satellite criterion $A(p) \mid p\#$ with failures `7, 17`, ternary rays $(3^d-1)/2$ (`1, 4, 121` at $d=1, 2, 5$, verified up to $d=40$), finite Euler on $D(360)$, and pentagonal series up to $t^{100}$;
   - `GATE-I08-A`, `GATE-I08-B` — CRT on $N=45$, oracle-free decomposition `60+1+16=77`, primary saturation, constructive 7-operation lift formula, and $\operatorname{lcm}$ gluing (`36 → 9`) on $N=36$, impulse and Fibonacci in Hankel, modular rank drop on $1^n+2^n+4^n$, and cubic $K_2(u) = (u^3 - 7u)/6$ over $\mathbb{F}_{5,7,11}$ and for primes $p \le 200$ with $\left(\frac{13}{p}\right) = 1$;
   - `GATE-I09-A`, `GATE-I09-B` — hollow triangle and 2-invisibility of $E_{\mathrm{even}}$, iterated contract pullback `64 → 16 = 16`, witness of a non-surjective seam without a global $\tau : K \to K$, phantom pairs `2 → 4` upon coarsening the seam, checkerboard octahedron of slices of $S_4$ with $\dim H^1 = 3$, Backbone Theorem `I.9.3a` ($\kappa = c_0 \oplus x_0 \oplus b_0 = 1 \iff \text{hollow face in }\mathcal{N}$) and presheaf $\mathcal{S}_w$, hollow tetrahedron $\partial\Delta_4$ with $H^2 \cong \mathbb{F}_2$, finite bound $|\mathrm{Par}(X,X)| = (n+1)^n$, memory refinement `2 → 4` upon expanding the library, and formulas of all canonical finite cardinalities `4..48`;
   - `GATE-I-SYS-SHA256`, `GATE-I-SYS-PROSE`, `GATE-I-SYS-SCALE`, `GATE-I-SYS-FLOAT` — integrity of all `11` canonical files of the `v3.3` volume (and `15` sealed laboratory primary sources when the external archive is present), absence of service stamps in the reader's prose prior to `### Verifiability Passport` and validity of all local links, completeness of volume of all ten chapters and presence of all `56` `TNR.BookI.*` cards and `23` `GR-*` passports, absence of `Float64`.

---

### II.2. Complete Registry of 56 Formal Specifications (Plan; Identifiers `TNR.BookI.*`)

All `56` formal prover targets below have the status **`status: planned`**: the column "Target type signature (`Lean 4` schema)" records the semantic mathematical specification of the statement, its exact premises, and carrier types for subsequent mechanization in `Lean 4 / Mathlib`, without claiming an already compiled `.olean` archive.

| 3 | `TNR.BookI.I01.three_state_counterexample` | `§I.1.0`, Counterexample `I.1.0a` (hidden move inside the zero fiber on 3 states) | `∃ (X : Type) (_ : Fintype X) (q : X → Bool) (T : X → X), Fintype.card X = 3 ∧ Function.Surjective q ∧ T ≠ id ∧ q ∘ T = q` | On `|X| = 2`, a surjective Boolean question is injective and `q ∘ T = q` forces `T = id`, but already on `|X| = 3` a permutation inside the two-point fiber is invisible to the window; `GATE-I01-A`. |

T : {p : L × R // πL p.1 = πR p.2} → {p : L × R // πL p.1 = πR p.2}, ...` | Under desynchronization `πL (TL l₀) ≠ πR (TR r₀)` on a compatible pair `(l₀, r₀) ∈ L ×_K R`, the image `(TL l₀, TR r₀)` leaves the seam subset in `L × R` (`8` inside `16`); `GATE-I01-B`. |

### II.3. Summary Certification Registry of the 57 Statements of Book I Across Five Epistemic Statuses

In the table below, for each of the `57` headings of theorems, lemmas, and propositions of Volume I, its unambiguous verbal epistemic status, classical foundation, specific contribution of the book, and verification gate in `11_verify_full_book_I_v3_3.jl` are given.

| # | Statement | Epistemic status | Classical foundation | Contribution of this book | `Julia` Gate |
| :-: | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `1` | **Theorem I.1.0a** | Classical result in operational notation | Partitioning a set by the kernel of a map $\mathrm{Eq}(q)$ and its invariance under injective renaming of the output scale $\tau \circ q$—elementary set theory. | Primacy of the partition $\mathrm{Eq}(q)$ over answer labels as the opening rule of the operational scene contract, together with a finite counterexample of coarsening under non-injective $\tau$. | `GATE-I01-A` |
| `2` | **Theorem I.1.0b** | Original proved result of this book | Boolean conditions on finite maps and pairwise counterexamples of axiom independence—classical axiomatic method. | Exact hierarchy and pairwise independence of the operational triads $(Z_D, Z_C, Z_F)$ and $(C_{\mathrm{div}}, C_{\mathrm{type}}, C_{\mathrm{step}})$ with minimal finite failure witnesses. | `GATE-I01-A` |
| `3` | **Lemma I.1.1** | Extension of a classical theorem within contract Γ | Canonical factorization of a map through its equivalence kernel and criterion for descending an operation onto a congruence—standard universal algebra. | Separating the set-theoretic pair kernel $\mathrm{Eq}(q)$ from the algebraic zero preimage $\ker_{\mathrm{alg}}(q)$ and explicit failure of quotient descent on the saturating semigroup $(\{0,1,2,3\}, \oplus_{\mathrm{sat}})$. | `GATE-I01-A` |
| `4` | **Theorem I.1.1** | Extension of a classical theorem within contract Γ | Joint map into the Cartesian product of two binary scales $\{0,1\}^2$ and counting four intersection cells—elementary finite combinatorics. | Criterion for the birth of the minimal four-state polar cell $S_4$ from a pair of independent binary questions and exact fraction `24/256` of question pairs on a four-point carrier. | `GATE-I01-B` |
| `5` | **Theorem I.1.2** | Original proved result of this book | The affine group $\mathbb{F}_2^2 \rtimes \mathrm{GL}_2(2)$, free transitive actions ($\mathbb{F}_2$-torsors), and Belnap's four-valued lattice $\mathcal{F}_4$—classical finite algebra and logic. | Simultaneous triad of equally rightful `2+2` partitions $(q_x, q_c, \beta = x \oplus c)$, mutual decoders $d_c, d_x$ on a fixed support, contrast between involutions $\sigma$ (`0` fixed points) and $\mathrm{swap}$ (`2`), and non-coincidence of Boolean difference with scalar difference on $\mathcal{F}_4$. | `GATE-I01-B` |
| `6` | **Theorem I.1.3** | Extension of a classical theorem within contract Γ | The construction $L \times_K R$ is the classical fibered product (pullback) in the category of sets $\mathbf{Set}$, and its universal property is standard. | Reading the pullback as a coupled whole of two observers sharing a seam ($8$ joint states of the cube $Q_3$ inside $16$ pairs of $S_4 \times S_4$) and criterion for unique descent of a compatible pair of steps. | `GATE-I01-B` |

## Part III. Appendix `AD` — Canonical File Integrity Schedule, Volume Summary, and Index `ATLAS`

### III.1. Integrity Verification of the Canonical Files of Volume I (`SHA-256`)

On every run, gate `GATE-I-SYS-SHA256` of the single-threaded verifier `11_verify_full_book_I_v3_3.jl` checks the presence, non-emptiness, and `64`-character `SHA-256` digests of all eleven canonical markdown files of Volume I (`00..10_*_V3_3_EN.md`) and records their byte-exact `SHA-256` hashes and word counts together with all `23` gate results in `12_book_I_v3_3_master_certificate.json`.

Key architectural and mathematical advances of edition `v3.3`:
1. **Unified monographic register across all ten chapters (`E0` + `I.1–I.9`):** every chapter builds its theorems from hand-worked finite models and closes with a strictly bounded historical-philosophical note.
2. **Integrated the two-engine methodological spine of `v3.3`:** the five-beat shift of the observer window $\Pi_k \to \Pi_{k+1}$ (external carrier vs. internal window $\to$ explicit shadow witness $(s,t) \in \ker m_{\mathrm{acc}}$ $\to$ splitting by an admissible continuation $\to$ three-way cross-check with a finite counterexample $\to$ crystallization of the next invariant) and the solution-count triad $|\mathcal{R}| \in \{1, >1, 0\}$ (forced canonical invariant / gauge freedom of scale / cohomological obstruction generating the next mathematical tier).
3. **Closed constructive and deductive links of Book I:** proved the general formula $C_{\min}(D_M) = 2M + 5$ ($M \ge 2, L \ge M$) and constructed in closed form the 19-state class `B` controller $Q_{19}$ via two $3 \times 3$ cyclic Latin squares (`Chapter I.4`), exhibited an explicit 7-state Markov counterexample of trace equivalence without strict lumpability (`Chapter I.3`), and eliminated all dummy loops and hardcoded constants in the verifier (`2O \subset \mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb{F}_9)`, equivariant decoding across `384` symmetries of $K_4$, 7-operation GCD lift, etc.).
4. **Cleaned the reader's canvas and verified scholarly attribution across all 57 statements:** zero service stamps appear prior to `### Verifiability Passport`, every classical structure is named explicitly with full primary-source bibliographic data, and `§I.9.5–I.9.7` synthesizes the nine-chapter $S_4$ backbone, the finite cardinality passport, and the threshold bridge to Book II.

---

### III.3. Cross-Chapter Multi-Aspect Index `ATLAS` of Book I (`v3.3`)

For rapid thematic and reference reading, four thematic axes across the entire First Volume are given below:

1. **Axis of Basic Definitions (`D-I.*`):**
   - *Operational contract of a scene $\Gamma$, question $q : X \to Y$, kernel $\mathrm{Eq}(q)$, five-beat protocol $\Pi_k \to \Pi_{k+1}$, triad $|\mathcal{R}| \in \{1, >1, 0\}$, and conditions $(Z_D, Z_C, Z_F)$, $(C_{\mathrm{div}}, C_{\mathrm{type}}, C_{\mathrm{step}})$* — `Introduction`, `Chapter I.1 (§I.1.0)`.
   - *Four-state polar cell $S_4$, comparison question $\beta = x \oplus c$, decoders $d_c, d_x$, and fibered seam $L \times_K R$* — `Chapter I.1 (§I.1.1–§I.1.3)`.
   - *Partial transitions $\mathbf{Par}$, modal preimages $\exists_R, \forall_R$, shadow boundary $\partial_\pi(S)$, and holonomy of the slice triangle $H_3$* — `Chapter I.2 (§I.2.1–§I.2.5)`.
   - *Complete protocol indistinguishability $x \sim_\Gamma y$, minimal future-memory quotient $X/{\sim_\Gamma}$, and dual tower of subalgebras $B_k$* — `Chapter I.3 (§I.3.1–§I.3.3)`.
   - *Closed feedback loop in a library $\Omega$, reversible trace recording $T_{\mathrm{rev}}$, cyclic Latin squares of the controller $Q_{19}$, and five-dimensional cost passport $\mathcal{C}$* — `Chapter I.4 (§I.4.1–§I.4.6)`.
   - *Endowment boundary `B1`: free module of signed contributions $\mathbb{Z}[X]$, incidence coboundary, quarter-turn operator $J_{\mathbb{Z}}$, and even Hadamard sublattice $\mathcal{L}$* — `Chapter I.5 (§I.5.1–§I.5.7)`.
   - *Adjacency-predicate graph $(V, R)$, linear bicycle code $\mathcal{B}$, Fano plane $\mathrm{PG}(2,2)$, surjection $\delta_4 = j^{-1} \circ \delta_7$ (`4096` sections), syndrome $\nu_W = J_7 \oplus \delta_7$, and Petersen graph $KG(5,2)$* — `Chapter I.6 (§I.6.1–§I.6.4)`.
   - *Divisor lattice $D(N)$, complementary divisor $\nu_N(d) = N/d$, equilibrium window $1+\pi(N)=N/2$, and prime satellite $A(p)$* — `Chapter I.7 (§I.7.1–§I.7.7)`.
   - *Oracle-free contract on a composite modulus $N = pq$, primary saturation, seven-step lift, and Hankel matrix $H(a)$* — `Chapter I.8 (§I.8.1–§I.8.5)`.
   - *Nerve of a window cover $\mathcal{N}$, simplicial obstruction groups $H^1, H^2$ over $\mathbb{F}_2$, and `Fold / Unfold` pair with trace ($U \circ F = \mathrm{id}_E$)* — `Chapter I.9 (§I.9.1–§I.9.6)`.

2. **Axis of Landmark Theorems (`T-I.*`):**
   - `Theorems I.1.0a–I.1.3` and `Lemma I.1.1` (primacy of $\mathrm{Eq}(q)$, criterion $|X/\mathrm{Eq}(q_{12})|=4$, bijections $d_c, d_x$, universal descent onto $L \times_K R$);
   - `Theorems I.2.1–I.2.4` and `Lemmas I.2.1–I.2.2` (Galois connection $\exists_R \dashv \forall_R$, Lawvere triad, Zhegalkin `16/8/6`, holonomy $H_3 = \mathrm{swap}^\kappa$, duality $F_\alpha / F_\alpha^\top$);
   - `Theorems I.3.1–I.3.3` and `Lemmas I.3.1–I.3.6` (minimality of $X/{\sim_\Gamma}$, Nerode stabilization in $\le \max(|X|-1, 0)$ steps, stochastic lumpability criterion and 7-state counterexample, classification `256 = 64 + 96 + 96`);
   - `Theorems I.4.1–I.4.3` and `Lemmas I.4.1–I.4.3` (reversible gate $T_{\mathrm{rev}}$, bound $C_{\min}(D_M) = 2M + 5$ and constructive controllers $Q_{11}, Q_{19}, Q_{22}$ with `139` commands, code `[6, 3, 3]_2`, history coupling, and cycle cost gauge);
   - `Theorems I.5.1–I.5.3` and `Proposition I.5.1` (free signed extension $\mathbb{Z}[X]$, shells of $T_3$, `Ban-Go` interference, quarter-turn $J_{\mathbb{Z}}$, and Hadamard parity);
   - `Theorems I.6.1–I.6.4` and `Lemma I.6.1` (four graphs on $A_6$ and $L(Q_3) \not\cong L(O_6)$, bicycle code, groups of order `48` with `19 / 13 / 1` involutions, Fano book and `4096` sections of $\delta_4$, Petersen and Klein quadric, permutation roots $J^2 = C_{2m}$ `0 vs 12`, and orbits `20 / 12 / 10`);
   - `Propositions I.7.1–I.7.2`, `Theorems I.7.1–I.7.3`, and `Lemma I.7.1` (Booleanness $D(N) \iff \mu(N)\neq 0$, equilibrium `{10, 12, 14}`, uniqueness of `(1, 2, 3)`, satellite criterion $A(p) \mid p\#$, ternary rays `1, 4, 121` and `[Ljunggren 1943]`, finite Euler identity);
   - `Theorems I.8.1–I.8.3`, `Lemmas I.8.1–I.8.3`, and `Proposition I.8.1` (CRT and oracle-free barrier, three regimes of divisor memory and 7-step lift, Kronecker's theorem on Hankel rank, modular rank drop, and cubic $K_2(u) = (u^3-7u)/6$);
   - `Propositions I.9.1–I.9.2`, `Theorems I.9.1–I.9.4`, `Lemma I.9.1`, and `Corollaries I.9.1–I.9.2` (hollow triangle and 2-invisibility of $E_{\mathrm{even}}$, associative contract gluing, $\dim H^1(\mathcal{N};\mathbb{F}_2) = 3$, backbone $\kappa = 1$ and presheaf $\mathcal{S}_w$, $H^2(\partial\Delta_4;\mathbb{F}_2) \cong \mathbb{F}_2$, dichotomy of reversible unfolding and Nerode compression).

3. **Axis of Canonical Counterexamples and Safeguards (`K-I.*`):**
   - `Introduction` — 2-cycle $F(x)=1-x$ without a fixed point; non-transitivity of tolerance $|a-b|\le 1$ on `0, 3/5, 6/5`; Cantor–Lawvere prohibition `0/16` and `0/256` surjections.
   - `Chapter I.1` — hidden permutation inside a two-point fiber on 3 states; failure of quotient descent of the saturating semigroup $(\{0,1,2,3\}, \oplus_{\mathrm{sat}})$; collision of scalar difference on the Belnap lattice $\mathcal{F}_4$.
   - `Chapter I.2` — non-surjective counterexample $\pi : \{0\} \to \{0,1\}$ for $\forall_\pi(S) \nsubseteq \exists_\pi(S)$; failure of Beck–Chevalley on the non-pullback `D = Empty`; `6/16` violations of naive contraposition under a partial step; twist of `H_3` at $\kappa = 1$.
   - `Chapter I.3` — idempotent without congruence; `16 vs 32` on $Q_3\times Q_3$ and `64 vs 65` on $Q_4\times Q_4$; 7-state chain with trace equivalence without lumpability; $\mathrm{TV} = 1$ under coinciding one-dimensional traces; point separation vs. measure separation (`24` pairs of rank `3 < 4`).
   - `Chapter I.4` — impotence of an open-loop tape of bijections on `Fin 2`; 2-bit error in the `[6, 3, 3]` code; asymmetry of the left reset zero.
   - `Chapter I.5` — destruction of associativity under `clamp_{[-1,1]}`; cancellation of two open paths in the `Ban-Go` cascade; unreachability of `(1, 1)` in $H(\mathcal{L})$ over $\mathbb{Z}$.
   - `Chapter I.6` — non-isomorphism $C_6 \not\cong 2C_3$ under equal vertex and edge counts; three non-isomorphic groups of order `48` with `19, 13, 1` involutions; failure of natural role descent $KG(5,2) \not\to O_6$; absence of permutation roots $J^2 = C_6$ in $\mathfrak{S}_6$.
   - `Chapter I.7` — absence of complements for middle floors of $D(12)$ and $D(45)$; failures of the satellite $A(p) \mid p\#$ at $p = 7$ and $p = 17$.
   - `Chapter I.8` — incompatibility of CRT on `(4, 6)`; oracle leak $r \bmod p$; irreversible loss of floor `25` from `(5, 5)` without $r$; forgetting $+1$ on the Hankel impulse and spurious zero $\Delta_2 \equiv 0 \pmod 7$; 4-cycle of the cubic $K_2$ at $p = 11$ and fixed points $u^2 \equiv 13 \pmod p$.
   - `Chapter I.9` — hollow triangle and 2-invisible parity $E_{\mathrm{even}}$; erasure of path multiplicity in relational composition `2 → 1`; unsolvability of an odd charge on the hollow tetrahedron despite $H^1 = 0$.

4. **Axis of Historical-Philosophical Notes with Boundary of Comparison (`M-I.*`, preparations for Chapter `III.3`):**
   - `Introduction (Part II)` — initial fork of classical Sāṃkhya (`SK 3, 19–21`), Patañjali's Yoga (`YS 1.2–1.4, 2.17, 2.20`), and Śaṅkara's Advaita Vedānta (`BSBh 1.1.1`) without syncretic conflation of schools.
   - `Chapter I.1 (§I.1.5)` — *sākṣitva* and *saṃyoga* (`SK 19–21`, `YS 2.17–2.20`) compared with the external position $\mathcal{O} \notin X$ and seam coupling.
   - `Chapter I.2 (§I.2.6)` — binding of attention to a chosen locus (*deśa-bandhaś cittasya dhāraṇā*, `YS 3.1`) compared with classical logic inside a single slice `L.1` and inter-regime holonomy `L.2`.
   - `Chapter I.3 (§I.3.6)` — outer gates of the present moment and the time-trivalent inner keeper (*dvārāṇi / dvārin*, `SK 33–35`; *vṛtti / saṃskāra*, `YS 1.18, 4.9`) compared with the future quotient $X/{\sim_\Gamma}$.
   - `Chapter I.4 (§I.4.7)` — specific functions of the three internal organs (`SK 29`), mutual prompting of *karaṇam* via *ākūta* (`SK 31`), and separating admissible kinematics $\Gamma$ from a distinct target specification $\gamma_{\mathrm{goal}}$.
   - `Chapter I.5 (§I.5.8)` — why the signed triad $\{-1, 0, +1\}$ of a single channel cannot be conflated with the three qualitatively distinct guṇas of Prakṛti (`SK 12–13`, cf. *dvandva* in `YS 2.48`).
   - `Chapter I.6 (§I.6.4)` — summary of the five functional roles of guṇas and instrument (`SK 12` and `SK 32`, strictly separated from *pañca-vāyavaḥ* in `SK 29`), four-member operation of `SK 30`, and strict mathematical prohibition of naive gluing $KG(5,2) \not\to O_6$.
   - `Chapter I.7 (§I.7.8)` — word-form generation tree with auxiliary markers *it / anubandha*, their procedural deletion *tasya lopaḥ* (`1.3.2–1.3.9`), and trace retention *pratyayalope pratyayalakṣaṇam* (`1.1.62`) in Pāṇini, plus rejection of numerological equation with Sāṃkhya lists (`13, 25, 50`).
   - `Chapter I.8 (§I.8.7)` — inference from a visible sign (*anumāna / liṅga / sāmānyato dṛṣṭam*, *Nyāya-sūtras* `1.1.5`, `SK 5–6`) and fallacious sign (*hetvābhāsa*) compared with the probe $\gcd(N, r)$ and Hankel rank.
   - `Chapter I.9 (§I.9.8)` — final exit of the dancer from the stage (*nartakī*, *viveka-khyāti*, `SK 59, 62–68`, separating the literal formula of `SK 64` *nāsmi na me nāham* from Gauḍapāda's commentary, and accounting for persistent contact *saty api saṃyoge* in `SK 66`) as the completion of the philosophical arc of Volume I prior to passing to the linear-operator and continuous axioms of Book II.

## Classical Mathematical and Historical-Scientific Sources of the Volume

The list below provides complete bibliographic data for the published classical works whose mathematical constructions and theorems are used in Volume I. The operational formulation of the scene contract, cross-chapter DOT constructions, and specific finite calculations of the book are not attributed to these authors.

1. C. E. Shannon. "A Mathematical Theory of Communication", *Bell System Technical Journal*, vol. 27, no. 3 (1948), pp. 379–423; no. 4 (1948), pp. 623–656: [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1002/j.1538-7305.1948.tb01338.x).
2. R. W. Hamming. "Error Detecting and Error Correcting Codes", *Bell System Technical Journal*, vol. 29, no. 2 (1950), pp. 147–160: [article text](https://zoo.cs.yale.edu/classes/cs323/doc/Hamming.pdf).
3. E. F. Moore. "Gedanken-Experiments on Sequential Machines", in C. E. Shannon, J. McCarthy (eds.), *Automata Studies*, Annals of Mathematics Studies, no. 34, Princeton University Press, Princeton, NJ, 1956, pp. 129–153: [article text](https://www.cs.cmu.edu/~cdm/resources/Moore1956-gedanken-experiments.pdf).
4. A. Nerode. "Linear Automaton Transformations", *Proceedings of the American Mathematical Society*, vol. 9, no. 4 (1958), pp. 541–544: [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1090/S0002-9939-1958-0135681-9).
5. J. G. Kemeny, J. L. Snell. *Finite Markov Chains*, D. Van Nostrand, Princeton, NJ, 1960 (reprinted: Undergraduate Texts in Mathematics, Springer-Verlag, New York, 1976): [publisher record](https://link.springer.com/book/9780387901923).
6. E. F. Codd. "A Relational Model of Data for Large Shared Data Banks", *Communications of the ACM*, vol. 13, no. 6 (1970), pp. 377–387: [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1145/362384.362685).
